\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{\mathbb{T}_{h}}\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}(U_{h})\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{V_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{W_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}(U_{h})\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{V_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L^{4}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{W_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|U_{h}\right\|_{L_{t,x}^{2}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{V_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{X_{h,+}^{0,\frac{1}{3}+}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{W_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{X_{h,+}^{0,\frac{1}{3}+}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u_{h}\|_{X_{h,+}^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|v_{h}\|_{X_{h,+}^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|w_{h}\|_{X_{h,+}^{-s,\frac{1}{2}}}.

For the second part of the
Z_{h,+}^{s}
-norm, we have from duality that

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\langle k\rangle^{s}\mathcal{F}_{t,x}\left(\nabla_{h}(u_{h}^{+}v_{h}^{+})\right)(\tau,k)}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle}\right\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\sup_{\|\widetilde{w_{h}}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{\infty}}\leq 1}\int_{\Theta}\sum_{\Delta}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})U_{h}(\tau_{1},k_{1})V_{h}(\tau_{2},k_{2})|\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau_{3},k_{3})|,

where
U_{h}
and
V_{h}
are defined as in (
4.7
) and

\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=\frac{|k_{3}|^{s}\left|\frac{2}{h}\sin(\frac{hk_{3}}{2})\right|}{|k_{1}|^{s}|k_{2}|^{s}\langle\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{2}-s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle}.

Case 1.

\max\limits_{i=1,2,3}\langle\tau_{i}-s_{h}(k_{i})\rangle=\langle\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle
. Note from Proposition
4.5
that

(4.12)

\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{|\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau,k)|}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L^{4}}\lesssim\left\|\frac{\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau,k)}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{2}{3}-}}\right\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{2}}\lesssim\|\widetilde{w_{h}}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{\infty}}.

Similarly as (
4.11
), the multiplier is bounded by

\frac{|k_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}+s}}{|k_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}+s}|k_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}+s}\langle\tau_{2}-s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle}\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle\tau_{2}-s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle}.

Then, by Proposition
4.5
and (
4.12
), we have

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{\Theta}\sum_{\Delta}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})U_{h}(\tau_{1},k_{1})V_{h}(\tau_{2},k_{2})|\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau_{3},k_{3})|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}(U_{h})\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{V_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L^{4}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{|\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau,k)|}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\|u_{h}\|_{X_{h,+}^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|v_{h}\|_{X_{h,+}^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|\widetilde{w_{h}}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{\infty}}.

Note by symmetry that the case
\max\limits_{i=1,2,3}\langle\tau_{i}-s_{h}(k_{i})\rangle=\langle\tau_{2}-s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle
follows analogously.

Case 2.

\displaystyle\max\limits_{i=1,2,3}\langle\tau_{i}-s_{h}(k_{i})\rangle=\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle
. By (
4.10
), we have

\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle\gtrsim|k_{1}|^{\frac{2}{3}}|k_{2}|^{\frac{2}{3}}|k_{3}|^{\frac{2}{3}}\langle\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle^{\frac{1}{6}}\langle\tau_{2}-s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{6}}.

Without loss of generality, we further assume that
|k_{1}|\leq|k_{2}|
. Then, the multiplier is bounded by

\frac{|k_{3}|^{1+s}}{|k_{1}|^{s+\frac{2}{3}}|k_{2}|^{s+\frac{2}{3}}|k_{3}|^{\frac{2}{3}}\langle\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle^{\frac{2}{3}}\langle\tau_{2}-s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{2}{3}}}\lesssim\frac{1}{|k_{1}|^{\frac{2}{3}}\langle\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle^{\frac{2}{3}}\langle\tau_{2}-s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{2}{3}}}.

Since both
\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{-\frac{2}{3}}
and
|k|^{-\frac{2}{3}}
are square integrable in
\tau
and
k
, respectively, we have

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},-\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},k_{1},k_{2},-k_{1}-k_{2})U_{h}(\tau_{1},k_{1})V_{h}(\tau_{2},k_{2})|\widetilde{w_{h}}(-\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},-k_{1}-k_{2})|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|k_{1}|^{\frac{2}{3}}}\|U_{h}(k_{1})\|_{L_{\tau}^{2}}\|V_{h}(k_{2})\|_{L_{\tau}^{2}}\|\widetilde{w_{h}}(-k_{1}-k_{2})\|_{L_{\tau}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\|U_{h}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{2}}\|V_{h}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{2}}\|\widetilde{w_{h}}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\|u_{h}\|_{X_{h,+}^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|v_{h}\|_{X_{h,+}^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|\widetilde{w_{h}}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{\infty}}.

∎

Lemma 4.8

(Bilinear estimate II)
.

Let
0\leq s\leq s^{\prime}\leq s+1
. Then,

\left\|\nabla_{h}(u_{h}^{\pm}\cdot e^{\pm\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}v_{h}^{\mp})\right\|_{Z_{h,\pm}^{s}}\lesssim h^{s^{\prime}-s}\|u_{h}^{\pm}\|_{X_{h,\pm}^{s^{\prime},\frac{1}{2}}}\|v_{h}^{\mp}\|_{X_{h,\mp}^{s^{\prime},\frac{1}{2}}},

for all
u_{h}^{\pm}\in X_{h,\pm}^{s^{\prime},\frac{1}{2}},v_{h}^{\mp}\in X_{h,\mp}^{s^{\prime},\frac{1}{2}}
.

Proof.

We only prove

\left\|\nabla_{h}(u_{h}^{+}\cdot e^{+\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}v_{h}^{-})\right\|_{Z_{h,+}^{s}}\lesssim h^{s^{\prime}-s}\|u_{h}^{+}\|_{X_{h,+}^{s^{\prime},\frac{1}{2}}}\|v_{h}^{-}\|_{X_{h,-}^{s^{\prime},\frac{1}{2}}}.

Similarly as in the proof of Lemma
4.7
, we write by duality that

(4.13)

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{h}(u_{h}^{+}\cdot e^{\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}v_{h}^{-})\right\|_{X_{h,+}^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\sup_{\|w_{h}\|_{X_{h,+}^{-s,-\frac{1}{2}}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\Theta}\sum_{\Delta}\frac{2}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{3}}{2}\right)\widetilde{u_{h}^{+}}(\tau_{1},k_{1})\widetilde{v_{h}^{-}}(\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}},k_{2})\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau_{3},k_{3})\right|

Define

(4.14)

\displaystyle U_{h}(\tau,k)

\displaystyle=\langle k\rangle^{s^{\prime}}\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\widetilde{u_{h}^{+}}(\tau,k)\right|

\displaystyle V_{h}(\tau,k)

\displaystyle=\langle k\rangle^{s^{\prime}}\langle\tau-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\widetilde{v_{h}^{-}}(\tau-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}},k)\right|

\displaystyle W_{h}(\tau,k)

\displaystyle=\langle k\rangle^{-s}\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau,k)\right|.

Then, the right-hand side of (
4.13
) is bounded by

(4.15)

\int_{\Theta}\sum_{\Delta}\mathcal{M}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})U_{h}(\tau_{1},k_{1})V_{h}(\tau_{2},k_{2})W_{h}(\tau_{3},k_{3}),

where

\mathcal{M}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=\frac{|k_{3}|^{s}\left|\frac{2}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{3}}{2}\right)\right|}{|k_{1}|^{s^{\prime}}|k_{2}|^{s^{\prime}}\langle\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

For
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}
, define the resonant function as

G(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=s_{h}(k_{1})-s_{h}(k_{2})+s_{h}(k_{3})+\frac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}.

Note that

G(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=\frac{8}{h^{3}}\cos\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{hk_{2}}{4}\right)\cos\left(\frac{hk_{3}}{4}\right).

From the identities

k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})+\tau_{2}-\frac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})+\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})=-G(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}),

we know that

\max_{j=1,2,3}|k_{j}|\sim\med_{j=1,2,3}|k_{j}|

and

(4.16)

\displaystyle M:=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\max\left(|\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})|,\left|\tau_{2}-\frac{2k_{2}}{h}+s_{h}(k_{2})\right|,|\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})|\right)

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{1}{h^{3}}\cos\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)\cos\left(\frac{hk_{3}}{4}\right)\left|\sin\left(\frac{hk_{2}}{4}\right)\right|.

Note that
\displaystyle\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
for all
k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}
. From (
4.16
), we immediately know

(1+M)^{\frac{1}{2}}\gtrsim\left(1+\frac{|k_{2}|}{h^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\gtrsim h^{-1}|k_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
M=|\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})|
in
\mathcal{M}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
. Note from (
4.16
) that dispersive smoothing effect is efficient when
|k_{2}|
is the maximum frequency, thus it suffices to consider the worst case when
|k_{2}|\leq|k_{1}|\sim|k_{3}|
. Since
h|k|\lesssim 1
for all
k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}
and
1+s-s^{\prime}\geq 0
, for non-zero frequencies, the multiplier
\mathcal{M}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
is bounded by

\frac{h|k_{3}|^{1+s}|k_{2}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{|k_{1}|^{s^{\prime}}|k_{2}|^{s^{\prime}}\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{h^{s^{\prime}-s}}{\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Then, by Parseval’s identity, Proposition
4.5
and Lemma
4.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:bi2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\Theta}\sum_{\Delta}\frac{h^{s^{\prime}-s}}{\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}U_{h}(\tau_{1},k_{1})V_{h}(\tau_{2},k_{2})W_{h}(\tau_{3},k_{3})

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{s^{\prime}-s}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{\mathbb{T}_{h}}\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}(U_{h})\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{V_{h}}{\langle\tau-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{W_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)dt,

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}(U_{h})\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{V_{h}}{\langle\tau-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L^{4}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{W_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{s^{\prime}-s}\|u_{h}^{+}\|_{X_{h,+}^{s^{\prime},\frac{1}{2}}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{V_{h}}{\langle\tau-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{X_{h,-}^{0,\frac{1}{3}}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{W_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{X_{h,+}^{0,\frac{1}{3}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{s^{\prime}-s}\|u_{h}^{+}\|_{X_{h,+}^{s^{\prime},\frac{1}{2}}}\|v_{h}^{-}\|_{X_{h,-}^{s^{\prime},\frac{1}{2}}}\|w_{h}\|_{X_{h,+}^{-s,\frac{1}{2}}}.

For the second part of
Z_{h,+}^{s}
, by duality, we have

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\langle k\rangle^{s}\mathcal{F}_{t,x}\left(\nabla_{h}(u_{h}^{+}\cdot e^{\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}v_{h}^{-})\right)(\tau,k)}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle}\right\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\sup_{\|\widetilde{w_{h}}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{\infty}}\leq 1}\int_{\Theta}\sum_{\Delta}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}){U_{h}}(\tau_{1},k_{1}){V_{h}}(\tau_{2},k_{2})|\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau_{3},k_{3})|,

where
U_{h}
and
V_{h}
are defined as in (
4.14
), and

\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=\frac{|k_{3}|^{s}\left|\frac{2}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{3}}{2}\right)\right|}{|k_{1}|^{s^{\prime}}|k_{2}|^{s^{\prime}}\langle\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle}.

Case 1.

M=|\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})|
. Similarly as above in addition to (
4.16
) under the worst case
|k_{2}|\leq|k_{1}|\sim|k_{3}|
,
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
is bounded by

\frac{h|k_{3}|^{1+s}|k_{2}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{|k_{1}|^{s^{\prime}}|k_{2}|^{s^{\prime}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{h^{s^{\prime}-s}}{\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Therefore, by Proposition
4.5
and (
4.12
), we have

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{\Theta}\sum_{\Delta}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})U_{h}(\tau_{1},k_{1})V_{h}(\tau_{2},k_{2})|\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau_{3},k_{3})|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle h^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}(U_{h})\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{V_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L^{4}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{|\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau,k)|}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle h^{s^{\prime}-s}\|u_{h}^{+}\|_{X_{h,+}^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|v_{h}^{-}\|_{X_{h,-}^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|\widetilde{w_{h}}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{\infty}}.

Note by symmetry that the case
M=|\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})|
follows analogously.

Case 2.

M=|\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})|
. By (
4.16
), we have

\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle\gtrsim h^{-\frac{4}{3}}|k_{2}|^{\frac{2}{3}}\langle\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle^{\frac{1}{6}}\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2}))\rangle^{\frac{1}{6}}.

Since the worst case occurs when
|k_{2}|
is the smallest one, we further assume that
|k_{2}|\leq|k_{1}|\sim|k_{3}|
. Then, the multiplier is bounded by

\frac{|k_{3}|^{1+s}h^{\frac{4}{3}}}{|k_{1}|^{s^{\prime}}|k_{2}|^{s^{\prime}+\frac{2}{3}}\langle\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle^{\frac{2}{3}}\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{2}{3}}}\lesssim\frac{h^{\frac{1}{3}+s^{\prime}-s}}{|k_{2}|^{\frac{2}{3}}\langle\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle^{\frac{2}{3}}\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{2}{3}}}.

Since both
\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{-\frac{2}{3}}
and
k^{-\frac{2}{3}}
are square integrable in
\tau
and
k
, respectively, we have

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},-\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},k_{1},k_{2},-k_{1}-k_{2})U_{h}(\tau_{1},k_{1})V_{h}(\tau_{2},k_{2})|\widetilde{w_{h}}(-\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},-k_{1}-k_{2})|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{h^{\frac{1}{3}+s^{\prime}-s}}{|k_{2}|^{\frac{2}{3}}}\|U_{h}(k_{1})\|_{L_{\tau}^{2}}\|V_{h}(k_{2})\|_{L_{\tau}^{2}}\|\widetilde{w_{h}}(-k_{1}-k_{2})\|_{L_{\tau}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle h^{\frac{1}{3}+s^{\prime}-s}\|U_{h}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{2}}\|V_{h}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{2}}\|\widetilde{w_{h}}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle h^{\frac{1}{3}+s^{\prime}-s}\|u_{h}^{+}\|_{X_{h,+}^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|v_{h}^{-}\|_{X_{h,-}^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|\widetilde{w_{h}}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{\infty}}.

∎

Lemma 4.9

(Bilinear estimate III)
.

Let
0\leq s\leq s^{\prime}\leq s+1
. Then,

\left\|\nabla_{h}(e^{\pm\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}u_{h}^{\mp}\cdot e^{\pm\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}v_{h}^{\mp})\right\|_{Z_{h,\pm}^{s}}\lesssim h^{\frac{1}{2}+s^{\prime}-s}\|u_{h}^{\mp}\|_{X_{h,\mp}^{s^{\prime},\frac{1}{2}}}\|v_{h}^{\mp}\|_{X_{h,\mp}^{s^{\prime},\frac{1}{2}}},

for all
u_{h}^{\mp},v_{h}^{\mp}\in X_{h,\mp}^{s^{\prime},\frac{1}{2}}
.

Proof.

In view of the proof of Lemma
4.8
, it suffices to prove

\left\|\nabla_{h}(e^{\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}u_{h}^{-}\cdot e^{\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}v_{h}^{-})\right\|_{Z_{h,+}^{0}}\lesssim h^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u_{h}^{-}\|_{X_{h,-}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}}\|v_{h}^{-}\|_{X_{h,-}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}}.

We start with the first part of
Z_{h,+}^{0}
norm

(4.17)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{h}(e^{\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}u_{h}^{-}\cdot e^{\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}v_{h}^{-})\right\|_{X_{h,+}^{0,-\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\sup_{\|w_{h}\|_{X_{h,+}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}}=1}\left|\int_{\Theta}\sum_{\Delta}\frac{2}{h}\sin(\tfrac{hk_{3}}{2})\widetilde{u_{h}^{-}}(\tau_{1}-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}},k_{1})\widetilde{v_{h}^{-}}(\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}},k_{2})\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau_{3},k_{3})\right|

Define

(4.18)

\displaystyle U_{h}(\tau,k)

\displaystyle=\langle\tau-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\widetilde{u_{h}^{-}}(\tau-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}},k)\right|

\displaystyle V_{h}(\tau,k)

\displaystyle=\langle\tau-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\widetilde{v_{h}^{-}}(\tau-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}},k)\right|

\displaystyle W_{h}(\tau,k)

\displaystyle=\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau,k)\right|.

Then, the right-hand side of (
4.17
) is bounded by

(4.19)

\int_{\Theta}\sum_{\Delta}\mathcal{M}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})U_{h}(\tau_{1},k_{1})V_{h}(\tau_{2},k_{2})W_{h}(\tau_{3},k_{3}),

where

\mathcal{M}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=\frac{\frac{2}{h}\left|\sin(\frac{hk_{3}}{2})\right|}{\langle\tau_{1}-\tfrac{2k_{1}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

For
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}
, define the resonant function as

G(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=s_{h}(k_{1})-\frac{2k_{1}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})-\frac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}-s_{h}(k_{3}).

Note that

G(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=-\frac{8}{h^{3}}\cos\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)\cos\left(\frac{hk_{2}}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{hk_{3}}{4}\right).

From the identities

k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\tau_{1}-\frac{2k_{1}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{1})+\tau_{2}-\frac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})+\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})=-G(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}),

we know that

\max_{j=1,2,3}|k_{j}|\sim\med_{j=1,2,3}|k_{j}|

and

(4.20)

\displaystyle M:=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\max\left(\left|\tau_{1}-\frac{2k_{1}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{1})\right|,\left|\tau_{2}-\frac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\right|,|\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})|\right)

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{1}{h^{3}}\cos\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)\cos\left(\frac{hk_{2}}{4}\right)\left|\sin\left(\frac{hk_{3}}{4}\right)\right|,

Note that
\displaystyle\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
for all
k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}
. From (
4.20
), we immediately know

(1+M)^{\frac{1}{2}}\gtrsim(1+M)^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq\left(1+\frac{|k_{3}|}{h^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq h^{-1}|k_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}},

which says that dispersive smoothing effect is efficient when
|k_{3}|
is the maximum frequency, thus it suffices to consider the worst case when
|k_{3}|\leq|k_{1}|\sim|k_{2}|
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
M=|\tau_{1}-\tfrac{2k_{1}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{1})|
in
\mathcal{M}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
. Since
h|k|\lesssim 1
for all
k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}
, for non-zero frequencies, the multiplier
\mathcal{M}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
is bounded by

\frac{h|k_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\frac{h^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Then, by Parseval’s identity, Proposition
4.5
and Lemma
4.2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\eqref{Dual integral2}\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{\Theta}\sum_{\Delta}\frac{h^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}U_{h}(\tau_{1},k_{1})V_{h}(\tau_{2},k_{2})W_{h}(\tau_{3},k_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle h^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{\mathbb{T}_{h}}\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}(U_{h})\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{V_{h}}{\langle\tau-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{W_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle h^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}(U_{h})\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{V_{h}}{\langle\tau-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L^{4}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{W_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle h^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u_{h}^{-}\|_{X_{h,-}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{V_{h}}{\langle\tau-\tfrac{2k}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{X_{h,-}^{0,\frac{1}{3}}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{W_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{X_{h,+}^{0,\frac{1}{3}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle h^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u_{h}^{-}\|_{X_{h,-}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}}\|v_{h}^{-}\|_{X_{h,-}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}}\|w_{h}\|_{X_{h,+}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}}.

Next, we consider the second part of the
Z_{h,+}^{s}
norm. By duality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mathcal{F}_{t,x}\left(\nabla_{h}(e^{\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}u_{h}^{-}\cdot e^{\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}v_{h}^{-})\right)(\tau,k)}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle}\right\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{\|\widetilde{w_{h}}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{\infty}}\leq 1}\int_{\Theta}\sum_{\Delta}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})U_{h}(\tau_{1},k_{1})V_{h}(\tau_{2},k_{2})|\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau_{3},k_{3})|,

where
U_{h}
and
V_{h}
are defined as in (
4.18
), and

\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=\frac{|\frac{2}{h}\sin\frac{hk_{3}}{2}|}{\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle\langle\tau_{1}-\tfrac{2k_{1}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Case 1.

M=|\tau_{1}-\tfrac{2k_{1}}{h}+s_{h}(k_{1})|
. Since
h|k|\lesssim 1
for all
k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}
, from (
4.20
), the multiplier
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
is bounded by

\frac{h|k_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle}\lesssim\frac{h^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle}.

Therefore, by Proposition
4.5
and (
4.12
), we have

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{\Theta}\sum_{\Delta}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\tau_{3},k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})U_{h}(\tau_{1},k_{1})V_{h}(\tau_{2},k_{2})|\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau_{3},k_{3})|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle h^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}(U_{h})\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{V_{h}}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L^{4}}\left\|\mathcal{F}_{t,h}^{-1}\left(\frac{|\widetilde{w_{h}}(\tau,k)|}{\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle h^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u_{h}^{-}\|_{X_{h,-}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}}\|v_{h}^{-}\|_{X_{h,-}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}}\|\widetilde{w_{h}}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{\infty}}.

Note by symmetry that the case
M=|\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})|
follows analogously.

Case 2.

M=|\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})|
. By (
4.20
), we have

\langle\tau_{3}-s_{h}(k_{3})\rangle\gtrsim h^{-\frac{3}{2}}|k_{3}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\langle\tau_{1}-\tfrac{2k_{1}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{1}))\rangle^{\frac{1}{8}}\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2}))\rangle^{\frac{1}{8}}.

Since
h|k|\lesssim 1
for all
(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}
, the multiplier is bounded by

\frac{h^{\frac{3}{2}}|k_{3}|^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\langle\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle^{\frac{5}{8}}\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{5}{8}}}\lesssim\frac{h^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|k_{2}|^{\frac{3}{4}}\langle\tau_{1}-s_{h}(k_{1})\rangle^{\frac{5}{8}}\langle\tau_{2}-\tfrac{2k_{2}}{h^{2}}+s_{h}(k_{2})\rangle^{\frac{5}{8}}}.

Since both
\langle\tau-s_{h}(k)\rangle^{-\frac{5}{8}}
and
k^{-\frac{3}{4}}
are square integrable in
\tau
and
k
, respectively, we have

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{h}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2},-\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},k_{1},k_{2},-k_{1}-k_{2})U_{h}(\tau_{1},k_{1})V_{h}(\tau_{2},k_{2})|\widetilde{w_{h}}(-\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},-k_{1}-k_{2})|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{h^{\frac{1}{2}}}{|k_{2}|^{\frac{3}{4}}}\|U_{h}(k_{1})\|_{L_{\tau}^{2}}\|V_{h}(k_{2})\|_{L_{\tau}^{2}}\|\widetilde{w_{h}}(-k_{1}-k_{2})\|_{L_{\tau}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle h^{\frac{1}{2}}\|U_{h}(k_{1})\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{2}}\|V_{h}(k_{2})\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{2}}\|\widetilde{w_{h}}(-k_{1}-k_{2})\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle h^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u_{h}^{-}\|_{X_{h,-}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}}\|v_{h}^{-}\|_{X_{h,-}^{0,\frac{1}{2}}}\|\widetilde{w_{h}}\|_{\ell_{k\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}}^{2}L_{\tau}^{\infty}}.

∎

As an immediate corollary, we have

Corollary 4.10
.

Let
0\leq s\leq s^{\prime}\leq s+1
. Suppose
u_{h}^{\pm}(t),v_{h}^{\pm}(t)\in Y_{h,\pm}^{s^{\prime},T}
are supported in
[0,T]
and satisfy mean zero assumption. Then, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{h}(u_{h}^{\pm}v_{h}^{\pm})\right\|_{Z^{s,T}_{h,\pm}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{6}-}\|u_{h}^{\pm}\|_{Y_{h,\pm}^{s,T}}\|u_{h}^{\pm}\|_{Y_{h,\pm}^{s,T}},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{h}(u_{h}^{\pm}\cdot e^{\pm\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}v_{h}^{\mp})\right\|_{Z^{s,T}_{h,\pm}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{s^{\prime}-s}T^{\frac{1}{6}-}\|u_{h}^{\pm}\|_{Y_{h,\pm}^{s^{\prime},T}}\|v_{h}^{\mp}\|_{Y_{h,\mp}^{s^{\prime},T}},

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{h}(e^{\pm\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}u_{h}^{\mp}\cdot e^{\pm\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}v_{h}^{\mp})\right\|_{Z_{h,\pm}^{s,T}}

\displaystyle\lesssim h^{\frac{1}{2}+s^{\prime}-s}T^{\frac{1}{6}-}\|u_{h}^{\mp}\|_{Y_{h,\mp}^{s^{\prime},T}}\|v_{h}^{\mp}\|_{Y_{h,\mp}^{s^{\prime},T}},

where all implicit constants are independent of
h>0
.

Proof.

For given
0<T\leq 1
, take
\eta\in C_{0}^{\infty}
as a non-negative cut-off function supported on
[-2,2]
and equals to
1
in
[-1,1]
such that

\|\eta_{T}(t)u_{h}^{\pm}\|_{Y^{s}_{h,\pm}}\leq 2\|u_{h}^{\pm}\|_{Y^{s,T}_{h,\pm}},

where
\eta_{T}(t)=\eta(\tfrac{t}{T})
. Then, by Lemma
4.7
9
9

9

In view of the proof, one can find at least
1/6
more dispersive smoothing effect.
and (
4.2
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{h}(u_{h}^{\pm}v_{h}^{\pm})\right\|_{Z^{s,T}_{h,\pm}}\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{h}((\eta_{T}u_{h}^{\pm})\cdot(\eta_{T}v_{h}^{\pm}))\right\|_{Z^{s}_{h,\pm}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle T^{\frac{1}{6}-}\|\eta_{T}u_{h}^{\pm}\|_{X_{h,\pm}^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}\|\eta_{T}u_{h}^{\pm}\|_{X_{h,\pm}^{s,\frac{1}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle T^{\frac{1}{6}-}\|u_{h}^{\pm}\|_{Y_{h,\pm}^{s,T}}\|u_{h}^{\pm}\|_{Y_{h,\pm}^{s,T}}.

The rest follow analogously.
∎

4.4.
Uniform bounds for coupled and decoupled FPU

Proposition 4.11

(Uniform bounds for coupled and decoupled FPU)
.

Let
s\geq 0
. For given initial data

(u_{h,0}^{+},u_{h,0}^{-})\in\mathbb{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})\quad\mbox{with}\quad h\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{h}}u_{h,0}^{\pm}=0,

there exist
T=T(\|(u_{h,0}^{+},u_{h,0}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})})
independent of
h\in(0,1]
and a unique solution
(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})
to the coupled FPU (
3.3
) ( resp.,
(v_{h}^{+},v_{h}^{-})
to the decoupled FPU (
3.5
) ) with

\displaystyle\left\|u_{h}^{\pm}(t)\right\|_{Y_{h,\pm}^{s,T}}\lesssim\|(u_{h,0}^{+},u_{h,0}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})},

\displaystyle\left(\text{resp.,}\left\|v_{h}^{\pm}(t)\right\|_{Y_{h,\pm}^{s,T}}\lesssim\|(u_{h,0}^{+},u_{h,0}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})}\right).

Proof.

For sufficiently small
0<T\ll 1
to be chosen later, consider the nonlinear map
\Phi=(\Phi^{+},\Phi^{-})
by

\displaystyle\Phi^{\pm}(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})

\displaystyle:=S_{h}^{\pm}(t)u_{h,0}^{\pm}

\displaystyle\mp\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{t}S_{h}^{\pm}(t-t^{\prime})\nabla_{h}\Big(u_{h}^{\pm}(t^{\prime})+e^{\pm\frac{2t^{\prime}}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}u_{h}^{\mp}(t^{\prime})\Big)^{2}dt^{\prime}.

Let
\mathbb{Y}_{h}^{s}
(analogously define
\mathbb{Y}^{s,T}_{h}
as a time localized version) denote the solution space for
(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})
equipped with the norm

\|(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{h}^{s}}^{2}:=\|u_{h}^{+}\|_{Y_{h,+}^{s}}^{2}+\|u_{h}^{-}\|_{Y_{h,-}^{s}}^{2}.

By Lemma
4.3
and Corollary
4.10
, we have

\left\|\Phi(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})\right\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{h,\pm}^{s,T}}\leq C\|(u_{h,0}^{+},u_{h,0}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{H}_{h}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})}+C^{\prime}T^{\frac{1}{6}-}\|(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{h}^{s,T}}^{2}.

We, here, emphasize that the constants
C
and
C^{\prime}
are independent of
h>0
. By taking
0<T<1
such that

(4.21)

CC^{\prime}T^{\frac{1}{6}-}\|(u_{h,0}^{+},u_{h,0}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{H}_{h}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})}<\frac{1}{4},

one verifies that
\Phi
maps from the set

\mathcal{Y}=\left\{(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})\in\mathbb{Y}_{h}^{s,T}:\|(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{h}^{s,T}}\leq 2C\|(u_{h,0}^{+},u_{h,0}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{H}_{h}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})}\right\}

to itself. The difference of two solutions can be treated similarly. Let
\mathcal{N}^{\pm}(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})(t)
denote

\mathcal{N}^{\pm}(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})(t)=\nabla_{h}\bigg\{\Big(u_{h}^{\pm}(t)\Big)^{2}+\Big(e^{\pm\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}u_{h}^{\mp}(t)\Big)^{2}+2u_{h}^{\pm}(t)e^{\pm\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}u_{h}^{\mp}(t)\bigg\}.

For
(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-}),(w_{h}^{+},w_{h}^{-})\in\mathcal{Y}
, we write

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{\pm}(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})-\mathcal{N}^{\pm}(w_{h}^{+},w_{h}^{-})

\displaystyle=\nabla_{h}\Big\{(u_{h}^{\pm}(t)+w_{h}^{\pm})(u_{h}^{\pm}(t)-w_{h}^{\pm})\Big\}+\nabla_{h}\Big\{\Big(e^{\pm\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}(u_{h}^{\mp}(t)+w_{h}^{\mp}(t))\Big)\Big(e^{\pm\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}(u_{h}^{\mp}(t)-w_{h}^{\mp}(t))\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle+\nabla_{h}\Big\{\Big(u_{h}^{\pm}(t)-w_{h}^{\pm}(t)\Big)e^{\pm\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}u_{h}^{\mp}(t)\Big\}+\nabla_{h}\Big\{u_{h}^{\pm}(t)e^{\pm\frac{2t}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}\big(u_{h}^{\mp}(t)-w_{h}^{\mp}(t)\big)\Big\},

which guarantees

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}T^{\frac{1}{6}-}(\|(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{h}^{s}}+\|(w_{h}^{+},w_{h}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{h}^{s}})\|(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})-(w_{h}^{+},w_{h}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{h}^{s,T}}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{2}\|(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})-(w_{h}^{+},w_{h}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{h}^{s,T}},

by taking the same
T>0
as in (
4.21
). Thus, we conclude that
\Phi
is contractive on
\mathcal{Y}
.
(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})
is a solution to (
3.3
) which, by uniqueness, coincides in
C_{t}([-T,T]:H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h}))
with the solution constructed in Proposition
3.11
.
∎

As a direct consequence of (
4.3
) and proposition
4.5
, we have

Corollary 4.12

(Uniform bounds for FPU solutions)
.

Let
s\geq 0
. For given initial data

(u_{h,0}^{+},u_{h,0}^{-})\in\mathbb{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})\quad\mbox{with}\quad h\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{h}}u_{h,0}^{\pm}=0,

Let
(u_{h}^{+}(t),u_{h}^{-}(t))
(resp.,
(v_{h}^{+}(t),v_{h}^{-}(t))
) be the solutions to the coupled FPU (
3.3
) (resp., to the decoupled FPU (
3.5
)) given in Proposition
4.11
. Then,

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left\|u_{h}^{\pm}(t)\right\|_{C([-T,T]:H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h}))}\lesssim\|u_{h,0}^{\pm}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})},\qquad\|u_{h}^{\pm}\|_{L^{4}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}_{h})}\lesssim\|u_{h,0}^{\pm}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})},

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left\|v_{h}^{\pm}(t)\right\|_{C([-T,T]:H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h}))}\lesssim\|u_{h,0}^{\pm}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})},\qquad\|v_{h}^{\pm}\|_{L^{4}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}_{h})}\lesssim\|u_{h,0}^{\pm}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})}.

4.5.
Decoupling the FPU system: Proof of Proposition
3.7

Proof of Proposition
3.7
.

Let
T=T(\|(u_{h,0}^{+},u_{h,0}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})})>0
be the common lifespan of solutions
(u_{h}^{+}(t),u_{h}^{-}(t))
to the coupled FPU and
(v_{h}^{+}(t),v_{h}^{-}(t))
to the decoupled FPU with initial data
(u_{h,0}^{+},u_{h,0}^{-})
constructed in Proposition
4.11
. Moreover, the solutions are uniformly (in
h
) bounded by the size of initial data

(4.22)

\|(u_{h}^{+},u_{h}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{h}^{s,T}},\|(v_{h}^{+},v_{h}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{Y}_{h}^{s,T}}\leq 2C\|(u_{h,0}^{+},u_{h,0}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})}.

By subtracting (
3.3
) from (
3.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle u_{h}^{\pm}(t)

\displaystyle-v_{h}^{\pm}(t)=\mp\int_{0}^{t}S_{h}^{\pm}(t-t^{\prime})\nabla_{h}\left\{(u_{h}^{\pm}(t^{\prime})+v_{h}^{\pm}(t^{\prime}))(u_{h}^{\pm}(t^{\prime})-v_{h}^{\pm}(t^{\prime}))\right\}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mp\int_{0}^{t}S_{h}^{\pm}(t-t^{\prime})\nabla_{h}\bigg\{\Big(e^{\pm\frac{2t^{\prime}}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}v_{h}^{\mp}(t^{\prime})\Big)^{2}+2v_{h}^{\pm}(t^{\prime})e^{\pm\frac{2t^{\prime}}{h^{2}}\partial_{h}}v_{h}^{\mp}(t^{\prime})\bigg\}dt^{\prime}.

By taking
Y_{h,\pm}^{0,T}
norm and then applying Corollary
4.10
to the quadratic terms, we estimate

\displaystyle\|u_{h}^{\pm}-v_{h}^{\pm}\|_{Y_{h,\pm}^{0,T}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}T^{\frac{1}{6}-}\Big(\|u_{h}^{+}\|_{Y_{h,+}^{0,T}}+\|u_{h}^{-}\|_{Y_{h,-}^{0,T}}\Big)\|u_{h}^{\pm}-v_{h}^{\pm}\|_{Y_{h,\pm}^{0,T}}

\displaystyle+C^{\prime}T^{\frac{1}{6}-}h^{\frac{1}{2}+s}\|v_{h}^{\mp}\|_{Y_{h,\mp}^{s,T}}^{2}+C^{\prime}T^{\frac{1}{6}-}h^{s}\|v_{h}^{\pm}\|_{Y_{h,\pm}^{s,T}}\|v_{h}^{\mp}\|_{Y_{h,\mp}^{s,T}},

for some
C^{\prime}>0
uniform in
h\in(0,1]
.
By shrinking the time interval, if necessary, such that

CC^{\prime}T^{\frac{1}{6}-}\|(u_{h,0}^{+},u_{h,0}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})}<\frac{1}{8},

we obtain from the uniform bounds of solutions (
4.22
) that

\|u_{h}^{\pm}-v_{h}^{\pm}\|_{Y_{h,\pm}^{0,T}}\lesssim h^{s}\|(u_{h,0}^{+},u_{h,0}^{-})\|_{\mathbb{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})}^{2}.

Then, the embedding
Y_{h,\pm}^{0,T}\hookrightarrow C_{t}([-T,T]:L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{h}))
gives the desired results.

∎

5.
Reformulations : Normal form reduction method

Due to the lack of smoothing effect, it cannot be shown directly that the decoupled system (
3.5
) converges to the KdV system (
3.6
) similarly as in
[
15
]
. Thus, we reformulate the decoupled FPU (
3.5
) using the argument in
[
1
]
as well as the corresponding KdV system (
3.6
).

By definitions of the Fourier transforms (
1.1
) and their inversion formula (
1.2
), the constant
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}
appears when taking the Fourier transform to
(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})^{2}
and
(\mathcal{W}^{\pm})^{2}
, see (
2.3
) and (
2.4
). Throughout this section, we drop the constant
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}
from (
2.3
) and (
2.4
) for the sake of convenience.

5.1.
Regularizing the FPU system via the Normal form approach

Let us recall the decoupled FPU (
3.5
) on
\mathbb{T}_{h}
with initial data
u_{h,0}^{\pm}
as

v_{h}^{\pm}(t)=S_{h}^{\pm}(t)u_{h,0}^{\pm}\mp\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{t}S_{h}^{\pm}(t-t^{\prime})\nabla_{h}\left(v_{h}^{\pm}(t^{\prime})\right)^{2}dt^{\prime},

where
S_{h}^{\pm}(t)=e^{\mp\frac{t}{h^{2}}(\nabla_{h}-\partial_{h})}
. The corresponding KdV system (
3.6
) on
\mathbb{T}
with initial data
\mathcal{L}_{h}u_{h,0}^{\pm}
is given by

w^{\pm}(t)=S^{\pm}(t)(\mathcal{L}_{h}u_{h,0}^{\pm})\mp\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{t}S^{\pm}(t-t^{\prime})\partial_{x}\left(w^{\pm}(t^{\prime})\right)^{2}dt^{\prime},

where
S^{\pm}(t)=e^{\mp\frac{t}{24}\partial_{x}^{3}}
. Note from Lemma
2.6
and Lemma
2.9
that

\|\mathcal{L}_{h}u_{h,0}^{\pm}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\|u_{h,0}^{\pm}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{h})},\;\text{ and }\;\int_{\mathbb{T}}\mathcal{L}_{h}u_{h,0}^{\pm}(x)\;dx=0.

Let us define a profile
\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}
by

(5.1)

\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k)=e^{\pm it\frac{1}{h^{2}}\left(\frac{2}{h}\sin(\frac{hk}{2})-k\right)}\widehat{v}_{h}^{\pm}(t,k),\quad\text{ equivalently, }\quad\mathcal{V}^{\pm}_{h}(t)=S_{h}^{\pm}(-t)v_{h}^{\pm}(t).

Note that

\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,0)=\widehat{v_{h}^{\pm}}(t,0)=h\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{h}}v_{h}^{\pm}(t,x)=0\;\text{ for all }t.

Then,
\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}
(indeed,
\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}
) solves the differential equation

(5.2)

\partial_{t}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k)=\mp\frac{1}{4}\frac{2i}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{2})=:\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{1}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k),

or the integral equation

(5.3)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k)

\displaystyle=\widehat{u_{h,0}^{\pm}}(k)\mp\frac{1}{4}\frac{2i}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}e^{\pm it^{\prime}\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{1})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{2})\;dt^{\prime},

where

(5.4)

\displaystyle\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{1}{h^{2}}\left\{\left(\frac{2}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)-k\right)-\left(\frac{2}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{2}\right)-k_{1}\right)-\left(\frac{2}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{2}}{2}\right)-k_{2}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle-\frac{8}{h^{3}}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{hk_{2}}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right).

Note that
\Phi_{h}^{2}
is symmetric over the variables. Let us define a bilinear form

\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{\frac{2i}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)}{i\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{2}).

Then, by symmetry, a direct computation gives

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k)=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\widehat{u_{h,0}^{\pm}}(k)-\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)+\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(0,k)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\partial_{t}\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime}.

Using (
5.2
) and (
5.4
), one has

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\partial_{t}\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{\frac{2i}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)}{i\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}e^{\pm it^{\prime}\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{1})\partial_{t}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{2})\;dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\mp\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{\frac{2i}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)}{i\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}e^{\pm it^{\prime}\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{1})

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\times\frac{2i}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{2}}{2}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{2}=k_{21}+k_{22}\\
k_{21}k_{22}\neq 0\end{subarray}}e^{\pm it^{\prime}\Phi_{h}^{2}(k_{2},k_{21},k_{22})}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{21})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{22})\;dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\\
k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}\neq 0\\
k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{\pm 2i\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)\cos\left(\frac{h(k_{2}+k_{3})}{4}\right)}{\frac{4}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)}e^{\pm it^{\prime}\Phi_{h}^{3}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{1})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{3})\;dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime},

where

(5.5)

\displaystyle\Phi_{h}^{3}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}):=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2}+k_{3})+\Phi_{h}^{2}(k_{2}+k_{3},k_{2},k_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle-\frac{8}{h^{3}}\sin\left(\frac{h(k_{1}+k_{2})}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{h(k_{2}+k_{3})}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{h(k_{3}+k_{1})}{4}\right).

Note that
\Phi_{h}^{3}
is symmetric over the variables. We further decompose the summation in
\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})
into two parts:

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\\
k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}\neq 0\\
k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0\end{subarray}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3},\;k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}\neq 0\\
k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0,\;(k_{1}+k_{2})(k_{1}+k_{3})=0\end{subarray}}\;+\;\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3},\;k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}\neq 0\\
(k_{1}+k_{2})(k_{2}+k_{3})(k_{1}+k_{3})\neq 0\end{subarray}},

and their corresponding parts of
\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})
are respectively denoted by
(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}
and
(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{NR}}
, thus, we write

(5.6)

\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)=(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)+(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{NR}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k).

Here, by the subscript
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{NR}
, we mean the resonant and nonresonant terms, respectively.

First, consider the resonant term
(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}
. The set of frequencies under

k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0,(k_{1}+k_{2})(k_{1}+k_{3})=0

can be divided into the following three sets

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{h,1}

\displaystyle=\{k_{1}+k_{2}=0\}\cap\{k_{1}+k_{3}=0\}\cap\{k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{h,2}

\displaystyle=\{k_{1}+k_{2}=0\}\cap\{k_{1}+k_{3}\neq 0\}\cap\{k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{h,3}

\displaystyle=\{k_{1}+k_{2}\neq 0\}\cap\{k_{1}+k_{3}=0\}\cap\{k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0\}.

Over
\mathcal{R}_{h,1}
, we know
k_{1}=-k_{2}=-k_{3}
and
k=-k_{1}
, which assure

(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)|_{\mathcal{R}_{h,1}}=\mp 2i\frac{\cos\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)}{\frac{4}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k)\right|^{2}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k),

due to
\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,-k)=\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k)}
, where
\overline{z}
,
z\in\mathbb{C}
, is the complex conjugate of
z
. Here
f|_{A}
denotes the restriction of
f
on the set
A
.

Under the symmetry over frequencies, we know

(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)|_{\mathcal{R}_{h,2}}=(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)|_{\mathcal{R}_{h,3}}.

Over
\mathcal{R}_{h,2}
, we know
k_{1}=-k_{2}\neq\pm k_{3}
and
k=k_{3}
, which assure

(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)|_{\mathcal{R}_{h,2}}=\pm 2i\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}\\
k_{1}\neq\pm k\end{subarray}}\frac{\cos\left(\frac{h(k-k_{1})}{4}\right)}{\frac{4}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)}|\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})|^{2}.

From the angle difference identity for the cosine function, and symmetry and anti-symmetry for cosine and sine functions, respectively, we have

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}\\
k_{1}\neq\pm k\end{subarray}}\frac{\cos\left(\frac{h(k-k_{1})}{4}\right)}{\frac{4}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)}|\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}\\
k_{1}\neq\pm k\end{subarray}}\frac{\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)\cos\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)+\sin\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)}{\frac{4}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)}|\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{h}{4}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}\\
k_{1}\neq\pm k\end{subarray}}|\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})|^{2},

which concludes

(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)|_{\mathcal{R}_{h,2}\cup\mathcal{R}_{h,3}}=\pm\frac{ih}{2}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}\\
k_{1}\neq\pm k\end{subarray}}|\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})|^{2}.

Collecting all, we obtain

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\mp 2i\frac{\cos\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)}{\frac{4}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k)\right|^{2}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\pm\frac{ih}{2}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}\\
k_{1}\neq\pm k\end{subarray}}|\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})|^{2}.

Now, we consider the nonresonant term
(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{NR}}
in (
5.6
). Let us define the (cubic) non-resonant set

(5.7)

\mathcal{A}(k)=\big\{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\in((\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*})^{3}:\;k=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3},\;k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}\neq 0,\;(k_{1}+k_{2})(k_{2}+k_{3})(k_{1}+k_{3})\neq 0\;\big\}

and a trilinear form

\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k):=\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\in\mathcal{A}(k)}\frac{2i\cos(\frac{hk}{4})\cos\left(\frac{h(k_{2}+k_{3})}{4}\right)}{\frac{4i}{h}\sin(\frac{hk_{1}}{4})\Phi_{h}^{3}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{3}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}\prod_{j=1}^{3}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{j}).

Then, a direct computation under the symmetry on
k_{2}
and
k_{3}
variables in the multiplier gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{NR}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)dt^{\prime}=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{h}{\textup{B}_{3}^{\pm}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(0,k)+\mathcal{F}_{h}{\textup{B}_{3}^{\pm}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}_{h}{\textup{B}_{3}^{\pm}}(\partial_{t}\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}_{h}{\textup{B}_{3}^{\pm}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\partial_{t}\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime}.

Using (
5.2
) and (
5.5
), one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{3}^{\pm}(\partial_{t}\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)

\displaystyle=\mp\frac{i}{2}\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{3})\in\mathcal{B}(k)}\frac{\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)\cos\left(\frac{h(k_{1}+k_{2})}{4}\right)\cos\left(\frac{h(k_{3}+k_{4})}{4}\right)}{\Phi_{h}^{3}(k,k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})}

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\times e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{4}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{3})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{4}),

where the set
\mathcal{B}(k)
of frequencies is given by

(5.8)

\mathcal{B}(k)=\left\{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in((\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*})^{4}:\begin{array}[]{l}k=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4},\;k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4}\neq 0,\\
(k_{1}+k_{2})(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{4})(k_{3}+k_{4})\neq 0\end{array}\right\},

and the quartic resonant function is given by

\Phi_{h}^{4}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})=\Phi_{h}^{3}(k,k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})+\Phi_{h}^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2},k_{1},k_{2}).\\

Similarly, we also have (by changing the variables)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\partial_{t}\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)

\displaystyle=\mp\frac{i}{4}\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{3})\in\mathcal{B}(k)}\frac{\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)\cos\left(\frac{h(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{4})}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{h(k_{1}+k_{2})}{2}\right)}{\sin\left(\frac{hk_{3}}{4}\right)\Phi_{h}^{3}(k,k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})}

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\times e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{4}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{3})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{4}).

Thus,

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{3}^{\pm}(\partial_{t}\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)+2\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\partial_{t}\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\mp\frac{i}{2}\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{3})\in\mathcal{B}(k)}\mathfrak{M}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{4}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{3})\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{4})

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{4}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k),

where

\mathfrak{M}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})=\frac{\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)\cos\left(\frac{h(k_{1+k_{2}})}{4}\right)\cos\left(\frac{h(k_{3}+k_{4})}{4}\right)}{\Phi_{h}^{3}(k,k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})}+\frac{\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)\cos\left(\frac{h(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{4})}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{h(k_{1}+k_{2})}{2}\right)}{\sin\left(\frac{hk_{3}}{4}\right)\Phi_{h}^{3}(k,k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})}

Collecting all, we conclude that

(5.9)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k)=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\widehat{u_{h,0}^{\pm}}(k)-\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)+\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(0,k)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}(\mathcal{F}_{h}\mathcal{N}_{h,3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{F}_{h}{\textup{B}_{3}^{\pm}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(0,k)+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{F}_{h}{\textup{B}_{3}^{\pm}}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t,k)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}_{h}\textup{B}_{4}^{\pm}(\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime}.

5.2.
Regularizing the KdV equation

We repeat the argument as in the previous subsection for KdV equation, already done by
[
1
]
, but we briefly arrange the computation for the sake of self-containedness and reader’s convenience.

Let us define a profile
\mathcal{W}^{\pm}
by

(5.10)

\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k)=e^{\mp it\frac{k^{3}}{24}}\widehat{w}^{\pm}(t,k),\quad\text{ equivalently, }\quad\mathcal{W}^{\pm}(t)=S^{\pm}(-t)w^{\pm}(t).

Note that

\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,0)=\widehat{w^{\pm}}(t,0)=\int_{\mathbb{T}}w^{\pm}(t,x)\;dx=0\;\text{ for all }t.

Then,
\mathcal{W}^{\pm}
(indeed,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}
) solves the differential equation

(5.11)

\partial_{t}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k)=\mp\frac{1}{4}(ik)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}e^{\pm it\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{2})=:\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{1}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k),

or the integral equation

(5.12)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathcal{L}_{h}u_{h,0}^{\pm}}(k)\mp\frac{1}{4}(ik)\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}e^{\pm it^{\prime}\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{1})\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{2})\;dt^{\prime},

where

(5.13)

\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})=-\frac{1}{24}(k^{3}-k_{1}^{3}-k_{2}^{3})=-\frac{1}{8}kk_{1}k_{2}.

Note that
\Psi^{2}
is symmetric over the variables. Let us define a bilinear form

\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{ik}{i\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}e^{\pm it\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{2}).

Then, by symmetry, a direct computation gives

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k)=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}_{h}u_{h,0}^{\pm}}(k)-\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k)+\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(0,k)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\partial_{t}\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime}.

Using (
5.11
) and (
5.13
), one has

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\partial_{t}\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{ik}{i\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}e^{\pm it^{\prime}\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{1})\partial_{t}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{2})\;dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\mp\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{ik}{i\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}e^{\pm it^{\prime}\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{1})

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\times(ik_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{2}=k_{21}+k_{22}\\
k_{21}k_{22}\neq 0\end{subarray}}e^{\pm it^{\prime}\Psi^{2}(k_{2},k_{21},k_{22})}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{21})\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{22})\;dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\\
k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}\neq 0\\
k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{\pm 2i}{k_{1}}e^{\pm it^{\prime}\Psi^{3}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{1})\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{3})\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k_{3})\;dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime},

where

(5.14)

\Psi^{3}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}):=\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2}+k_{3})+\Psi^{2}(k_{2}+k_{3},k_{2},k_{3})=-\frac{1}{8}(k_{1}+k_{2})(k_{2}+k_{3})(k_{1}+k_{3}).

Note that
\Psi_{h}^{3}
is symmetric over the variables. We further decompose the summation in
\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})
into two parts:

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\\
k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}\neq 0\\
k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0\end{subarray}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3},\;k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}\neq 0\\
k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0,\;(k_{1}+k_{2})(k_{1}+k_{3})=0\end{subarray}}\;+\;\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3},\;k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}\neq 0\\
(k_{1}+k_{2})(k_{2}+k_{3})(k_{1}+k_{3})\neq 0\end{subarray}},

and their corresponding parts of
\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})
are respectively denoted by
(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}
and
(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{NR}}
, thus, we write

(5.15)

\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)=(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)+(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{NR}}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k).

Here, by the subscript
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{NR}
, we mean the resonant and nonresonant terms, respectively.

First, consider the resonant term
(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}
. The set of frequencies under

k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0,(k_{1}+k_{2})(k_{1}+k_{3})=0

can be divided into the following three sets

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}

\displaystyle=\{k_{1}+k_{2}=0\}\cap\{k_{1}+k_{3}=0\}\cap\{k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}

\displaystyle=\{k_{1}+k_{2}=0\}\cap\{k_{1}+k_{3}\neq 0\}\cap\{k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{3}

\displaystyle=\{k_{1}+k_{2}\neq 0\}\cap\{k_{1}+k_{3}=0\}\cap\{k_{2}+k_{3}\neq 0\}.

Over
\mathcal{R}_{1}
, we know
k_{1}=-k_{2}=-k_{3}
and
k=-k_{1}
, which assure

(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k)|_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}=\mp\frac{2i}{k}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k)\right|^{2}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k).

Over
\mathcal{R}_{2}
, we know
k_{1}=-k_{2}\neq\pm k_{3}
and
k=k_{3}
, which assure

(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k)|_{\mathcal{R}_{2}}=\pm 2i\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}\\
k_{1}\neq\pm k\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{k_{1}}|\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})|^{2}=0,

due to the symmetric in
k_{1}
. Similarly, we have

(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k)|_{\mathcal{R}_{3}}=\pm 2i\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}\\
k_{1}\neq\pm k\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{k_{1}}|\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})|^{2}=0,

Collecting all, we obtain

(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k)=\mp\frac{2i}{k}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k)\right|^{2}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k).

Now, we consider the nonresonant term
(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{NR}}
in (
5.15
). Let us define the (cubic) non-resonant set

\Lambda(k)=\big\{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:\;k=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3},\;k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}\neq 0,\;(k_{1}+k_{2})(k_{2}+k_{3})(k_{1}+k_{3})\neq 0\;\big\}

and a trilinear form

\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k):=\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\in\Lambda(k)}\frac{2i}{ik_{1}\Psi^{3}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}e^{\pm it\Psi^{3}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}\prod_{j=1}^{3}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{j}).

Then, a direct computation under the symmetric on
k_{2}
and
k_{2}
variables in the multiplier gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{NR}}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)dt^{\prime}=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\mathcal{F}{\textup{D}_{3}^{\pm}}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(0,k)+\mathcal{F}{\textup{D}_{3}^{\pm}}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}{\textup{D}_{3}^{\pm}}(\partial_{t}\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle-2\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}{\textup{D}_{3}^{\pm}}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\partial_{t}\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime}.

Using (
5.11
) and (
5.14
), one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{3}^{\pm}(\partial_{t}\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k)

\displaystyle=\mp\frac{i}{2}\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{3})\in\Gamma(k)}\frac{1}{\Psi^{3}(k,k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})}

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\times e^{\pm it\Psi^{4}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{3})\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{4}),

where the set
\Gamma(k)
of frequencies is given by

\Gamma(k)=\Bigg\{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}:\begin{array}[]{l}k=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4},\;k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4}\neq 0,\\
(k_{1}+k_{2})(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{4})(k_{3}+k_{4})\neq 0\end{array}\Bigg\},

and the quartic resonant function is given by

\Psi^{4}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})=\Psi^{3}(k,k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})+\Psi^{2}(k_{1}+k_{2},k_{1},k_{2}).\\

Similarly, we also have (by changing the variables)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\partial_{t}\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k)

\displaystyle=\mp\frac{i}{2}\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{3})\in\Gamma(k)}\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}}{k_{3}\Psi^{3}(k,k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})}

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\times e^{\pm it\Psi^{4}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{3})\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{4}).

Thus,

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{3}^{\pm}(\partial_{t}\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k)+2\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{3}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\partial_{t}\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\mp\frac{i}{2}\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{3})\in\Gamma(k)}\frac{2(k_{1}+k_{2})+k_{3}}{k_{3}\Psi^{3}(k,k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})}e^{\pm it\Psi^{4}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})}\prod_{j=1}^{4}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k_{j})

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{4}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k).

Collecting all, we conclude that

(5.16)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t,k)=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}_{h}u_{h,0}^{\pm}}(k)-\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k)+\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{2}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(0,k)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}(\mathcal{F}\mathcal{N}_{3}^{\pm})_{\mathcal{R}}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{F}{\textup{D}_{3}^{\pm}}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(0,k)+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{F}{\textup{D}_{3}^{\pm}}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t,k)

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{4}^{\pm}(\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm},\mathcal{W}^{\pm})(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime}.

Remark 5.1
.

Comparing (
5.16
) with (
5.9
), the resonant term

\mp\frac{2i}{k}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k)\right|^{2}\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime}

corresponds to

\mp 2i\frac{\cos\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)}{\frac{4}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k)\right|^{2}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t^{\prime},k)\;dt^{\prime},

while the rest

\pm\frac{ih}{2}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}\in(\mathbb{T}_{h})^{*}\\
k_{1}\neq\pm k\end{subarray}}|\widehat{\mathcal{V}_{h}^{\pm}}(t,k_{1})|^{2}\;dt^{\prime}

itself is negligible in
L^{2}
due to an additional
h
.

6.
Continuum limit of the Decoupled FPU system to the KdV system

6.1.
KdV equation

In this section, we are going to introduce some interesting results concerned with KdV equation.

Lemma 6.1

(
L^{4}
-Strichartz estimates
[
7
]
)
.

For
f\in X^{0,\frac{1}{3}}_{\tau=k^{3}}
, we have

\|f\|_{L^{4}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\|f\|_{X_{\tau=k^{3}}^{0,\frac{1}{3}}}.

Proof.

The proof is analogous in the proof of Proposition
4.5
, and it can be found in
[
7
]
as well as
[
32
,
33
,
27
]
.
∎

Proposition 6.2

(
L^{2}
well-posedness
[
7
]
)
.

The KdV system

(6.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w_{\pm}\pm\frac{1}{24}\partial_{x}^{3}w_{\pm}\mp\frac{1}{4}\partial_{x}(w_{\pm}^{2})

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle w_{\pm}(0)

\displaystyle=w_{\pm,0}.

is well-posed in
L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
.

Remark 6.3
.

Low regularity results for KdV equation have been extensively studied. We, for instance, refer to
[
24
,
10
,
23
]
.

As an immediate corollary, we have

Corollary 6.4

(Uniform bounds for KdV solutions)
.

Let
s\geq 0
. For given initial data
w_{\pm,0}\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
, let
w_{\pm}(t)
be the solutions to the KdV system (
6.1
) given in Proposition
6.2
. Then,

\left\|w_{\pm}(t)\right\|_{C([-T,T]:H_{x}^{s}(\mathbb{T}))}\lesssim\|w_{\pm,0}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T})},\qquad\|w_{\pm}\|_{L^{4}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T})}\lesssim\|w_{\pm,0}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T})}.

Proof.

It follows from Proposition
6.2
and Lemma
6.1
.
∎

6.2.
Some preliminaries

For
M\in\mathbb{N}
, let
P_{\leq M}
denote the projection operator on
L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
defined by

P_{\leq M}f(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sum_{|k|\leq M}\widehat{f}(k).

With this, we define
P_{\geq M}
and
P_{L\leq\cdot\leq M}
by

P_{\geq M}=I-P_{<M}\quad\mbox{and}\quad P_{L\leq\cdot\leq M}=P_{\leq M}-P_{<L},

where
I
is the identity operator on
L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
.

We first address some preliminaries.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
M>0
. Then,

(6.2)

\displaystyle\|P_{\leq M}\textup{D}_{1}^{\pm}(f_{1},f_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim M^{\frac{3}{2}}\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})},

(6.3)

\displaystyle\|P_{\geq M}\textup{D}_{2}^{\pm}(f_{1},f_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{M}\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})},

(6.4)

\displaystyle\|P_{\geq M}\textup{D}_{3}^{\pm}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{3}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}

(6.5)

\displaystyle\|P_{\geq M}\textup{D}_{4}^{\pm}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{3}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{4}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})},

for
f_{j}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
,
1\leq j\leq 4
.

Proof.

(
6.2
) just follows from the duality and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

For (
6.3
), we know

|\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{2}^{\pm}(f_{1},f_{2})(k)|\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=k_{1}+k_{2}\\
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|k_{1}k_{2}|}\big|\hat{f}_{1}(k_{1})\big|\big|\hat{f}_{2}(k_{2})\big|.

Since
k=k_{1}+k_{2}
with
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0
, we have
M\leq|k|\lesssim\max(|k_{1}|,|k_{2}|)
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
|k_{1}|\geq|k_{2}|
, Using
|k|\leq|k_{1}(k-k_{1})|
, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and the summation of
|k|^{-2}
over
|k|\geq M
, one has

\displaystyle\|P_{\geq M}\textup{D}_{2}^{\pm}(f_{1},f_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left(\sum_{|k|\geq M}\left|\sum_{k_{1}\neq 0}\frac{1}{|k_{1}(k-k_{1})|}|\hat{f}_{1}(k_{1})\hat{f}_{2}(k-k_{1})|\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{1}{M}\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}.

For (
6.4
), let
\widehat{F}_{1}(k_{1})=|k_{1}|^{-1}|\widehat{f}_{1}(k_{1})|
and
\widehat{F}_{j}(k_{j})=|k_{j}|^{-\frac{1}{4}}|\widehat{f}_{j}(k_{j})|
,
j=2,3
. From

|(k_{1}+k_{2})(k_{1}+k_{3})(k_{2}+k_{3})|\gtrsim\max(|k_{1}|,|k_{2}|,|k_{3}|,|k|)\gtrsim|k_{2}|^{\frac{1}{4}}|k_{3}|^{\frac{1}{4}}|k|^{\frac{1}{2}},

on
\Lambda(k)
, we have

\displaystyle\|P_{\geq M}\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{3}^{\pm}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left(\sum_{|k|\geq M}\left|\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\in\Lambda(k)}\frac{2i}{ik_{1}\Psi^{3}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}e^{\pm it\Psi^{3}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})}\prod_{j=1}^{3}\widehat{f}_{j}(k_{j})\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{1}{M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\left\|\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\in\Lambda(k)}\widehat{F}_{1}(k_{1})\widehat{F}_{2}(k_{2})\widehat{F}_{3}(k_{3})\right\|_{\ell_{k}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{1}{M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|F_{1}F_{2}F_{3}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{1}{M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|F_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T})}\|F_{2}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{T})}\|F_{3}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{1}{M^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{3}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}.

For (
6.5
), observe that

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{2(k_{1}+k_{2})+k_{3}}{k_{3}(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{4})(k_{3}+k_{4})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{2}{k_{3}(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{4})(k_{3}+k_{4})}-\frac{1}{(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{4})(k_{3}+k_{4})},

which yields

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{F}\textup{D}_{4}^{\pm}(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4})(k)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{3})\in\Gamma(k)}\frac{1}{k_{3}(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{4})(k_{3}+k_{4})}\prod_{j=1}^{4}\widehat{f}_{j}(k_{j})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\sum_{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{3})\in\Gamma(k)}\frac{1}{(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{4})(k_{3}+k_{4})}\prod_{j=1}^{4}\widehat{f}_{j}(k_{j}).

By duality, it suffices to show

(6.6)

\sum_{\Pi_{1}(k)}\frac{\widehat{f}_{1}(k_{1})\widehat{f}_{2}(k_{2})\widehat{f}_{3}(k_{3})\widehat{f}_{4}(k-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3})\widehat{g}(-k)}{k_{3}(k-k_{3})(k-k_{1}-k_{2})}\lesssim\prod_{j=1}^{4}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}

and

(6.7)

\sum_{\Pi_{2}(k)}\frac{\widehat{f}_{1}(k_{1})\widehat{f}_{2}(k_{2})\widehat{f}_{3}(k_{3})\widehat{f}_{4}(k-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3})\widehat{g}(-k)}{(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})(k-k_{3})(k-k_{1}+k_{2})}\lesssim\prod_{j=1}^{4}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}

where
\Pi_{1}(k)
and
\Pi_{2}(k)
are the sets of frequencies given by

\Pi_{1}(k)=\{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in(\mathbb{Z})^{4}:k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}=k,k_{3}(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{4})(k_{3}+k_{4})\neq 0\}

and

\Pi_{2}(k)=\{(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4})\in(\mathbb{Z})^{4}:k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}=k,(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{4})(k_{3}+k_{4})\neq 0\},

respectively. By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mbox{LHS of }\eqref{eq:quartiliniear-1}\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\Pi_{1}(k)}\frac{|\widehat{f}_{1}(k_{1})\widehat{f}_{3}(k_{3})|^{2}}{|(k-k_{3})(k-k_{1}-k_{2})|^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{\Pi_{1}(k)}\frac{|\widehat{f}_{2}(k_{2})\widehat{f}_{4}(k-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3})\widehat{g}(-k)|^{2}}{|k_{3}|^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{3}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{4}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}

and

\displaystyle\mbox{LHS of }\eqref{eq:quartiliniear-2}\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\Pi_{2}(k)}\frac{|\widehat{f}_{1}(k_{1})\widehat{f}_{3}(k_{3})|^{2}}{|(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})(k-k_{3})|^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{\Pi_{2}(k)}\frac{|\widehat{f}_{2}(k_{2})\widehat{f}_{4}(k-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3})\widehat{g}(-k)|^{2}}{|k-k_{1}-k_{2}|^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{3}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|f_{4}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\|g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}.

∎

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
0<h\leq 1
and
0\leq\alpha\leq 1
. Let

\mathcal{M}_{h,0}^{2,\pm}(t,k,k_{1},k_{2})=ke^{\pm it\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}-\frac{8}{h^{3}k^{2}}\sin^{3}\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}.

Then, for
|k|,|k_{1}|,|k_{2}|\leq\frac{\pi}{h}
, we have

|\mathcal{M}_{h,0}^{2,\pm}(t,k,k_{1},k_{2})|\lesssim|k|^{1+\alpha}h^{\alpha}(1+|t|)^{\alpha}\left(|k_{1}k_{2}|\max(|k|,|k_{1}|,|k_{2}|)\right)^{\alpha}.

Proof.

By the mean-value theorem, the Taylor remainder theorem, and (
2.7
) (for
\alpha=1
), we have

\displaystyle\left|e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}-e^{\pm it\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle|t|\left|\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})-\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle|t|\left|\frac{4}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)-k\right|\left|\frac{2}{h^{2}}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{hk_{2}}{4}\right)\right|

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+|t|\left|\frac{4}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)-k_{1}\right|\left|\frac{k}{2h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{2}}{4}\right)\right|

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+|t|\left|\frac{4}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{2}}{4}\right)-k_{2}\right|\left|\frac{kk_{1}}{8}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle|t|h(|k|+|k_{1}|+|k_{2}|)|kk_{1}k_{2}|.

Interpolating with the following trivial bound

\left|e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}-e^{\pm it\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\right|\lesssim 1,

one has

(6.8)

\left|e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}-e^{\pm it\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\right|\lesssim\left(h|t||kk_{1}k_{2}|\max(|k|,|k_{1}|,|k_{2}|)\right)^{\alpha},

for
0\leq\alpha\leq 1
. For any
0\leq\alpha\leq 1
, collecting (
2.7
), (
2.8
) and (
6.8
), we conclude that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{M}_{h,0}^{2,\pm}(t,k,k_{1},k_{2})|\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left|\frac{2}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)-k\right|\left|\frac{4}{h^{2}k^{2}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\right|

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+|k|\left|\frac{4}{h^{2}k^{2}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)-1\right|\left|e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\right|

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+|k|\left|e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}-e^{\pm it\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle|k||hk|^{\alpha}+|k|\left(h|t||kk_{1}k_{2}|\max(|k|,|k_{1}|,|k_{2}|)\right)^{\alpha},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
0<h\leq 1
and
0\leq\alpha\leq 1
. Let

\mathcal{M}_{h,1}^{2,\pm}(t,k,k_{1},k_{2}):=\frac{k}{\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}e^{\pm it\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}-\frac{8\sin^{3}\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)}{h^{3}k^{2}\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}

Then, for
|k|,|k_{1}|,|k_{2}|\leq\frac{\pi}{h}
with
k_{1}k_{2}\neq 0
, we have

|\mathcal{M}_{h,1}^{\pm}(t,k,k_{1},k_{2})|\lesssim\frac{h^{\alpha}(1+|t|^{\alpha})}{|k_{1}k_{2}|}\left(|kk_{1}k_{2}|\max(|k|,|k_{1}|,|k_{2}|)\right)^{\alpha}.

Proof.

By (
2.8
), we know

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{4}{h^{2}k^{2}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)-1\right)\frac{\frac{2}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)}{\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\right|\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle(h|k|)^{\alpha}\left|\frac{\frac{4}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)}{\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle(h|k|)^{\alpha}\left|\frac{h^{2}}{\sin\left(\frac{hk_{1}}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{hk_{2}}{4}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{(h|k|)^{\alpha}}{|k_{1}k_{2}|},

for any
0\leq\alpha\leq 2
. Thus, it suffices to deal with

(6.9)

\left|\frac{k}{\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}e^{\pm it\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}-\frac{2\sin\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)}{h\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\right|.

Let
\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{j}=\frac{4}{h}\sin\left(\frac{hk_{j}}{4}\right)
,
j=1,2
. Then, it is known that
|\mathcal{K}_{j}|\sim|k_{j}|
,
j=1,2
. Note that similarly as (
2.7
), we have

(6.10)

\left|\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)-1\right|\lesssim(h|k|)^{\alpha},

for any
0\leq\alpha\leq 2
. By (
2.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)}{\mathcal{K}_{1}\mathcal{K}_{2}}-\frac{1}{k_{1}k_{2}}\right|=

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left|\frac{\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)(k_{1}-\mathcal{K}_{1})k_{2}}{\mathcal{K}_{1}\mathcal{K}_{2}k_{1}k_{2}}+\frac{\left(\cos\left(\frac{hk}{4}\right)-1\right)\mathcal{K}_{1}k_{2}}{\mathcal{K}_{1}\mathcal{K}_{2}k_{1}k_{2}}+\frac{\mathcal{K}_{1}\left(k_{2}-\mathcal{K}_{2}\right)}{\mathcal{K}_{1}\mathcal{K}_{2}k_{1}k_{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left|\frac{(h|k_{1}|)^{\alpha}}{\mathcal{K}_{1}\mathcal{K}_{2}}\right|+\left|\frac{(h|k|)^{\alpha}}{\mathcal{K}_{2}k_{1}}\right|+\left|\frac{(h|k_{2}|)^{\alpha}}{\mathcal{K}_{2}k_{1}}\right|

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{h^{\alpha}}{|k_{1}k_{2}|}\left(|k|^{\alpha}+|k_{1}|^{\alpha}+|k_{2}|^{\alpha}\right),

for any
0\leq\alpha\leq 2
. Thus, by (
6.8
), we obtain for
0\leq\alpha\leq 1
that

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:M1-1}\leq

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\left|\frac{k}{\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\left(e^{\pm it\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}-e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\right)\right|

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+\left|\left(\frac{2\sin\left(\frac{hk}{2}\right)}{h\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}-\frac{k}{\Psi^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\right)e^{\pm it\Phi_{h}^{2}(k,k_{1},k_{2})}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\frac{1}{|k_{1}k_{2}|}\left(h|t||kk_{1}k_{2}|\max(|k|,|k_{1}|,|k_{2}|)\right)^{\alpha}+\frac{h^{s}}{|k_{1}k_{2}|}\left(|k|^{\alpha}+|k_{1}|^{\alpha}+|k_{2}|^{\alpha}\right),

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
0<h\leq 1
and
